# PROGRESS

**The one file that answers "where am I".** House rule 1: one piece of state has
exactly one owner. This is the owner of *current position*. Nothing else may
restate it — other files reference a gate id, they never say which gate is active.

Read this first, every session. Update it before you close, in the same commit as
the work (house rule 6).

It was removed from MIPS III and later architecture levels. Note that in MIPS IV the COP3 primary opcode was reused for the COP1X instruction class.」* **COP3 是 MIPS I 與 II，MIPS III 拿掉，MIPS IV 收走 opcode。** 結論沒變（這顆是 MIPS-I，`Config.M = 0` 量），理由換掉。`SPEC.md` 在問的事。**清單上一條都沒拿掉**：`probe3` 的 `--isa` 命中維持 **13（8 `mfc3` ＋ 5 `cache`）**，八條現在叫 `mfc3`／`MIPS-I COP3`，而且逐條印出位址與解碼（`mfc3 v0,$0` … `$7`）而不是 `.word`。③ **一個誤讀，三個後果，三處都改**：`isa_hit` 的標籤；`reads()` 回 `{rs, rt}` —— 那是 **COP1X 的**運算元模型，`mtc3` 的 rs 欄是 4 **因為 4 是 MT 選擇碼**，舊版把那個 4 當成 `$a0` 讀，所以 `lw a0` 接 `mtc3 t0,$0` 會被報成一個不存在的違規；`control_flow` 不知道 `bc3` 是分支，delay slot 裡的 load 只查得到 fall-through 一條腿 —— **和 `M8` 15。舊的 `reads()` 回 `{rs, rt}`，於是工具把一個**子運算碼選擇欄**當成剛被載入的暫存器，報了一個不存在的 hazard。那個字在 `0x802BA660` 以上（`CPU-44` 記的 code／data 分界），**是資料被當成程式碼解**。🔴 **所以正確的說法不是「什麼都沒動」，而是：這棵樹裡唯一看得見這個修法的地方就是那個 `CP3 $0`／`$1`、發 `0x010`、再把 `0x002C0000`＋`0x1FFF`（**8 KiB**）寫進 `$4`／`$5`。**名字與行為互相解釋，而兩個尺寸與資料表的 I-MEM／D-MEM 逐一吻合。** **又是 `arch/rlx/` 那個失誤**：命名檔就在樹裡，而「還開著的路是一份會講的文件」這句話是在那份文件已經在磁碟上時寫的。③ 🔴 **`CCTL` 是 0→1 邊緣觸發的** —— 寫一個已經是 1 的位元不做任何事，所以「寫一次就期待有效果」的探針是一支**不會失敗的工具**。`rlx_cctl` 本來就 clear／write／clear，加上 `CPU-39` 量到讀側恆為 0，RLX4181 的 read-modify-write 形式在這顆上退化成普通寫入 —— 兩件事對上了。④ 🔴 **`CPU-25` 11,467 行 grep 只命中交換器 MAC 表）。⑤ 🔴 **`docs/rlx-cache-and-cp0.md` §② 的「cell E 讓 cell A 可判讀」太強**：E 只在 write-back 那個分支裡是 A 的正控制；E 若量到 write-through，它對常駐與否一句話都沒說，**而剩下的「不 read-allocate」與「別名被 snoop」只有真的 bus master 分得開**。對 `R6` 它們等價，前提要等 `R6` 用真引擎重測。⑥ **量了一件本來以為要花一次上機的事**：`stage2.bin` 的**程式區一條 COP3 都沒有**（97 個 opcode `0x13` 命中全部 ≥ `0x8040A5B8` 且全是 ASCII），所以 loader 是對著 **reset 預設**的 BASE/TOP 發那兩個命令。⚠️ | **Last session but fourteen** | 🔴 **2026-08-25, at the bench — `R1g-4b`, the second seating. One power cycle, 23 captures, four prediction blocks, zero flash bytes, and `R1e` CLOSES.** `bench/2026-08-25b/`. **16 of 16 captures written before the block that predicted them; every one of the 16 byte counts exact** (`reply-size.py`, whose sweep over `bench/` now reads 135 modelled / 0 unexplained). ① 🔴 **`CPU-27` is measured: `Status = 1000FC00`, `BEV = 0`.** The vectors are in RAM at `0x80000080` **and the core fetches there** — `break` trapped into our handler and came back (`break.count=1`, `cause=00000024` = ExcCode 9, `break.epc=80500270`, the exact address of the `break` in the emitted image). 🆕 The withdrawn `1000FC01` was right about every bit **except `IEc`**, and `IEc=0` is what *`J` clears `IE`* predicts for a payload — vindicated with a mechanism rather than resurrected. ② 🔴 **`CPU-04`'s value is measured: `PRId = 0000CD01`**, and the prediction written into `PREDICTIONS-b4-block1.md` before the run hit exactly. **The value is 量 and the NAME is not** — nothing here maps `0xCD01` to `RLX4181` rather than `RLX5281`, so **do not write `RLX5281`**; what is missing is a source, not a measurement. ③ 🔴 **`F50b` is answered and the answer is the expensive one: `Count` is not implemented.** rd 9 reads `00000000` `S_ZERO`, `count.before = count.after = count.delta = 0`. **So `R5-0`'s SoC timer driver is a PREREQUISITE, not a bonus, and `R1c` loses its first timing route** — one of the four decisions this gate exists to make. ④ 🔴 **`nowrite = 0` across all 256 rows is what makes that zero real**: `mfc0` always writes `rt` on this core, so *the destination was never written* is excluded **by the instrument built to detect it**. Must-fix 4's question, answered. ⑤ 🔴 **The CP0 ignores the select field**: `moves = 8` and all eight are `rd 1`'s selects, `rows.suppressed = 0`, `rows.printed = 39`. R3000-class decode, measured. ⑥ 🔴 **`Random` (rd 1) is the positive control this census could not otherwise have** — sixteen values across its eight rows, index field **5 … 29, all inside 0 … 31**, which **corroborates `CPU-08`'s 32 TLB entries by a route with no TLB probe in it**. Without a register that *does* move on this die, *`Count` does not move* would have been unfalsifiable; qemu's `S_MOVES` is a control on qemu. ⑦ 🔴 **CP0 20 (`CCTL`) is separated at last**: `probe1`'s `XCT0` read `0` and could not tell *reads zero* from *destination not written*; with `nowrite=0` it can. **CP0 20 is a write-only command register that reads back zero** — the sentence `R5b`'s MTD driver needs. ⑧ **`Config` reads `00000000`** → `Config.M = 0`, **not a MIPS32 core**, proved outright — **and there is no `Config1`, so `CPU-25` cannot come out of CP0.** ⑨ **`traps = 0`** on all 256: an unimplemented CP0 read returns zero and does not trap. ⑩ **`rfe` balances the KU/IE stack** — `status_end == status` exactly, derived before the run from the R3000 shift rule. ⑪ **Two channels and every control fired**: all 40 header words identical, seal `EC84408D` agreeing, poison margin 8/8 `DEADC0DE` with un-poisoned DRAM beyond it, `H2h-utlb` byte-identical to **this seating's** `H0c`, `H2i-below` proving `probe2` wrote nothing below its block. 🔴 **And the false alarm the seating avoided is measured**: against 2026-08-25's `H0c` it *differs*, because the UTLB vector is DRAM bias and this DRAM's bias moves 4–27 bits per 256 across power cycles. ⑫ 🔴 **Five of my predictions were refuted and all five are kept**, the expensive one being the last: **a second `J 80500000` booted the vendor kernel**, because **the loader re-stages `0x80500000` on a watchdog reset too** — so a payload cannot be re-run on one power cycle without re-uploading, which nothing had written down. Cost: one power cycle; nothing earned was lost. ⑬ **Three tool defects, found by pointing the tools at the new captures**: `reply-size.py check` crashed on the one input it exists to report (`UNREADABLE` could never print) — 12 cases → **21**; `boot-timeline.py`'s artifact anchor assumed a one-byte prefix and read **4.2 ms** where tonight's two-byte prefix should give 342.5 — pooled spread 149.1 % → **2.5 %**, 12 cases → **15**; `exc.S`'s comment had `0x110F0000` for `0x10F00000`. `RUNSHEET.md` § Results B4 `R1g-4b`; `SPEC.md` `CPU-04`/`CPU-08`/`CPU-25`/`CPU-27`/`CPU-36`–`CPU-42`/`MEM-15`/`REG-13`/`LDR-22`/`CLK-15`; `bench/2026-08-25b/` |
| **Last session but fifteen** | 🔴 **2026-08-25, desk only, fourth sitting — `R1g-4b`'s desk half: all five must-fix items are in `probe2`, and the suite that has to be able to tell the fixed payload from the shipped one went 66 cases to 106.** ① 🔴 **The four audit items and the fifth that `R1g-4a` added are done, and three of them got stronger than the list asked.** `SAFE_A0` before `break` (the macro moved to a new `rlxasm.h` because `exc.S` needs it too) — and widening `S1` *to every instruction that could fault*, which is what the entry actually says, found **four more unguarded `mfc0`s in `uart.S`**, so those got the guard and `rlx_fault_frame` moved to `report.c`. `install_handler` reads all 44 words back through KSEG1 and refuses rather than hanging. `RLX_FLUSH_ISC` is gone, not stamped: **`ISC` is per payload with `override`**, so `make P=probe2 ISC=1` cannot bring it back. The compiled-in `0x0BEBC200` is right to **24 ppm**, inside a normal crystal's tolerance; both old numbers stay in `RUNSHEET.md` `E2` with the reason they fell. ② 🔴 **`C-14` closes on a bit that moved rather than on an absence**: `PABCD_DAT` bit 5 `1`→`0` under the held button, `CNR` and `DIR` unchanged, no `Booting...` — a GPIO, not `RESET#`. **`D3` is retired before it was spent**, and `notes/power-and-programmer.md` §3's *"this unit's loader polls only the UART"* is refuted by the same three cells. ③ 🔴 **`0x81000000` is not scratch**: `C7-pre` read a live 32-byte-periodic descriptor table at `0x81000400` — `next = prev = &next` at `0x81000418`, and uninitialised DRAM cannot produce its own address — and `C7-pre2` a second live structure at `0x81800000` pointing to 28.04 MiB. **That refutes `RUNSHEET.md` §C's premise**, which argued from the address being far above the loader's image and the staged kernel: part one wrote onto a live structure and nothing broke, and that was luck. `§G`'s upload address moves to `0x80A00000`, chosen by a probe (`G4-addr-probe`) instead of by an argument, and `D2b` gains a third outcome. ④ **Three intermediate conclusions of mine were retracted, each by a cell that had named the outcome in advance**: *"`PSRP` bit 8 is sticky"* (by `E11c2`, which read a port whose jack was empty so no new down-event was possible — bit 8 went 1→0 in one read, and `NET-11` and the datasheet were right); *"`PSRP1` has no jack"* (by `E11e` — the jack count had been reported at the bench, not measured); *"`CONT`'s silence is residue in the line buffer"* (by `flush-cont`, which returned a bare prompt with no `Unknown command !`). `tools/check-predictions.py` is what makes *in advance* checkable at all — four prediction blocks, written `+8.2 s` … `+650.4 s` before the captures they name. ⑤ Free and unasked: `NET-13` closes with the physical jack order **`0, 2, 3, 4, 1`**, so a driver taking the port index from the silkscreen position is wrong on exactly one jack; `PSRP` bits 6 and 5 are the negotiated flow control (`ANLPAR` `0xC1E1` → `0xCDE1`, XOR `0x0C00`, and the `PSRP` pair moves with it and nothing else does); `0xB8000000` reads `0x8196E001`, the SoC naming itself while its own loader prints `chipName: UNKNOWN`; and a `DW` reply is structurally `len(cmd) + 2 + 47 × lines + 9` bytes, which predicted six reply sizes exactly, every one before its cell ran. `bench/2026-08-24b/`, `RUNSHEET.md` Results and Results — B2, `docs/loader-command-semantics.md` §f, `docs/loader-phy-and-switch.md` §7, `notes/power-and-programmer.md` §3, `bench/README.md` |

An adversarial review of `hazlint` 1.0 (four lenses, every claim put to a separate agent whose job was to refute it) returned **17 confirmed of 24 claimed**. ① 🔴 **`K4`, the population control, built its words with a private helper, so it exercised a code path no scanned file ever took** — it could not have caught any defect in span construction and it looked exactly like it could. It now goes through `raw_span()`, the same call `--raw` makes. ② The `lwl`/`lwr` pair exemption skipped the whole successor instead of the `rt` merge alone, so it swallowed the successor's **base** register — and since `reads()` did not model the merge either, **the exemption had only ever suppressed true positives**. ③ Six span defects: two sections of a `.o` both report `sh_addr == 0` and collided in the address map; a flat word list made the last word of one span the predecessor of the first word of the next; a span running past EOF was silently shortened. ④ Scan coverage: a section whose `sh_offset` is past EOF vanished from the scan *and* the report; only `PF_X` segments were scanned with nothing saying what was not. ⑤ Decoder: the four REGIMM branch-likely forms and the coprocessor branches were not recognised as branches at all. **`1474 / 646 / 0` is unchanged through all of it.** `hazlint` is 1.1; the suite went 42→56 with a mutation per fix, because the review's finding 6 was that the old suite could not tell the patched tool from the shipped one. It also answers `C7`'s own question: one line carries 12 words, so a 1 KiB probe needs 22 lines not 15 — R1's no-network path is 47% dearer. ② **`A2` is closed by changing the instrument, not the cell** — `console-capture.py --esc` streams ESC from inside the capture loop, so the pre-prompt stream is kept. ③ **`A0` is a new cell** and it measured that re-opening the serial port does not reset the board (`BRD-09`'s DTR inference → measurement); its first attempt failed on queued ESC, which `SPEC.md` `LDR-16` already knew — **the rule was in the table and not in the procedure, so it was rediscovered rather than followed**, and it is now in the procedure. ④ `EW` rounds an unaligned address **up**, `EB` does not; one loader, two write primitives, opposite handling. ⑤ **`§D` was stopped before it ran**: `D1`+`D2`+`D2b` need one capture that sends a command *then* streams ESC across the reboot, and `--esc` runs before `--send`. 2026-08-28 on entering it, the day `R2a/b/d` closed. **The lists below it are kept,
open ones first: `R1h` (open), then `R2a/b/d` and `R1-gate` (closed)** — the column
*"where it is most likely to be wrong"* is only worth having if it can be read
afterwards against what actually went wrong. 🔄 **It used to read
`plan/DAY-ZERO.md` items 0–8, and that was a second owner of this state** —
`DAY-ZERO` owns the instruments that had to exist before any gate could run, a
gate owns its own steps. ⚠️ **`R1h`'s list is still live, not history**: 🔄 **`R1h-2` closed 2026-08-29
with `R3-7` — they are the same step — so what is live is `R1h-3`, which shares
`R3-8`'s seating, and `R1h-4`, which follows it.** *(This sentence paired a
closed step with a closed step until 2026-08-29.)*

### The four things this file tracks, because they are not the same thing

This line existed to name one of them and was read as naming all four. **Active
step above is a bench session, which is not one of `DAY-ZERO` items 0–8** — that
is not an error, but nothing said so.

| | what it is | where it lives | answers |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Gates** `S0`, `R0`…`R9`, `P1`…`P4` | the fifteen milestones | the board below | *how far is the project* |
| **`DAY-ZERO` items 0–8** | 🔄 **the instruments that had to exist before *any* gate could run** — not "the desk step list for the gate that is active now", which is what this row said until 2026-08-25 and which made it a **second owner** of the state § *Step list* owns. `item 7` had already drawn the line the row missed: `hazlint` is `DAY-ZERO`'s, the hazard verdicts it enables are `R1b`'s | `plan/DAY-ZERO.md` | *what had to exist before the first gate* |
| **§ Step list** 🆕 | the session-sized steps of the gate that is active now, written on entering it (`plan/SESSIONS.md` §0b) | **this file**, above | *what do I do next at the desk* |
| **Runsheet sessions** `B1`, `B2`, `B3` | what gets typed at the bench. 🔄 **Not "one power cycle each", which is what this row said until 2026-08-24**: `B1` and `B2` share one; `B3` costs **two of its own**, because `G6`'s and `G7`'s jumps each end with a kernel running and the loader gone; and a session outlives a seating — `B1` has now been typed across three power cycles, one directory each | `RUNSHEET.md` | *what do I do next at the device* |
| **`C-n`** | open questions that outlive one session, each owned by a gate | Carried forward, below | *what is still unanswered* |

**Runsheet sessions are not plan items and never were.** They exist because
`DAY-ZERO` items 2c and 4 produced a large number of claims read out of a flash
dump, a datasheet and four vendors' source, and **no number in this repository
has been measured on the device**. A session appears when enough desk claims
have piled up to be worth one power cycle. `B1` and `B2` are the first, and
`B3` is the one that closes the gate that is active.

A `C-n` is *not* extra work appearing from nowhere either: it is the mechanism
for **not** doing work now. When a session turns up a question it cannot close,
the choice is to chase it (and lose the thread) or to write it down with the
gate that owns it. The list is long because the desk work has been productive,
and every row names where it gets settled.

## Step list for the active gate — `R3`

**Written 2026-08-28 on entering the gate, before any of it was done.** Budget
**猜, uncalibrated: 12 steps.** The gate board says 12 and `plan/` §12.1 says 13
(8 desk + 4 bench + 1 instrument). Neither is calibrated and the difference is
not worth resolving before the first step reports. **Four of the twelve are at
the bench and they are not four seatings** — `R1h-3` shares the first one, and
`probe3` runs first inside it.

🔴 **This gate is the first one whose DoD is a sentence about the device rather
than about a file, and that is why it is decomposed before it is attempted.**
*"My kernel boots to a shell and pings"* reads as one claim and is five, each
with its own observable and its own way of being faked.

### The DoD, split into what can be refuted

| | claim | the observable | what refutes it |
|:-:|---|---|---|
| **D1** | the image is delivered and entered | `---Jump to address=80500000` followed by output that is **not** the loader's | silence after the jump line — which is two causes, not one (`RUNSHEET` §B3 says so already) |
| **D2** | the decompressor runs and the kernel is entered | `Linux version 2.6.30.9 (<me>@<host>) … <date>` | the banner is the vendor's build stamp → **the vendor kernel booted, not mine** (see the anti-DoD below); or output stops inside the decompressor |
| **D3** | early bring-up completes | `MemTotal:` printed, and the console echoes | a panic before `start_kernel`, or a hang with no further output — the case the plan calls *memory-related*, and where it also says the DRAM is the loader's so the suspect is the map or the cache setup |
| **D4** | userspace is reached and the shell accepts typing | a prompt, **and a typed command returns output** | `Kernel panic - not syncing: No init found`, or a prompt that does not echo |
| **D5** | it pings | `ping -c 4` from the board gets ≥ 1 reply **and** the host's capture shows the echo request | replies with no host-side capture = something else answered; host-side requests with no replies = the driver transmits and does not receive |

🔴 **The anti-DoD, and it is written first because this project has already been
caught by it.** 2026-08-25, `R1g-4b`: *a second `J 80500000` booted the vendor
kernel*, because **the loader re-stages `0x80500000` on a watchdog reset**. So a
banner is not evidence that my kernel ran. **Every pass criterion below carries a
discriminator the vendor image cannot produce**: the `(user@host)` and date in
`Linux version`, plus one string emitted by code that exists only in my tree.
A capture without that string is recorded as *unattributed*, never as a pass.

### The two decisions this gate opens with, both made at `R3-0`

**Decision A — `R3` builds with `rsdk-1.3.6-4181`, through its own wrapper.**
It fills the half of `TC-05` that was left blank.

1. 讀 `TC-14`: it is the **only wrapper on hand that accepts `-march=4181`**, and
   `boards/rtl8196e/config.in` is `ARCH_CPU_RLX4181=y`.
2. 讀 `TC-15`: `{4180, 4181, 5181, mips1}` is the side that exposes the load delay
   slot and gets the padding; one `vmlinux` from one source through
   `rsdk-1.5.5`'s wrapper carries **21,185** load-use violations. That is a
   disqualification, not a preference.
3. 讀 `TC-17`: the drop's own top-level `.config` selects this release for this
   board.
4. 讀 `TC-16`: it is one of two toolchains that has linked a **complete
   `vmlinux`** for this board, rc=0 — and the other one is the one point 2
   disqualifies.
5. 🔴 **The objection that does NOT carry over, said plainly so the decision is
   not defended with it.** The reason to fear `rsdk-1.5.5` driving `-march=4181`
   is that its uClibc and crt are 5281-built and put three load-use violations
   into every program's entry path (`notes/rebuild-vs-shipped.md` §4). **A kernel
   links neither.** What is left against that route is that its flag set is a
   **reconstruction (推)** rather than a reading, and that is enough on its own:
   if `R3` does not boot, the vendor's own (toolchain, board, config) triple
   leaves the code generator out of the suspect list, and a reconstructed
   toolchain puts it back in.

**Decision B — the first boot mounts an initramfs built from this unit's own
userspace, not the flash rootfs.**

1. 🔴 **Safety dominates on a one-device project.** ~~An initramfs boot never
   instantiates an MTD partition map.~~ 🔴 **REFUTED on the device, 2026-08-29.**
   量, `bench/2026-08-30b/L3.log`: the `loud` boot created **two** MTD
   partitions, and the first spans `0x000000–0x130000`, which **contains both
   regions `CLAUDE.md` forbids**. Nothing was written and the decision still
   stands — on its other three legs, which were always the stronger ones — but
   **the safety margin this leg claimed was never there.** `notes/kernel-build.md`
   §16 owns the reading. *(Kept as written, because a decision's stated reason is
   worth more when it can be read against what happened.)* The flash-root route needs me to author
   one, and the two regions `CLAUDE.md` forbids — `0x000000–0x005FFF` and
   `0x006000–0x007FFF` — are inside the part a wrong map covers. 讀:
   `rtkload/Makefile` computes `FLASH_OFFSET=30000` under `CONFIG_RTL_819X`
   while this unit's kernel header is at flash `0x060000`, so **the drop's layout
   is already known not to be this unit's** and the map would be new work rather
   than reuse.
2. 讀: it is **the vendor's own supported path**, not an exotic one —
   `boards/rtl8196e/Makefile`'s header is *"Build instructions for Realtek RLXOCP
   with initramfs"* and `rtkload/Makefile` branches on `CONFIG_BLK_DEV_INITRD`.
3. **It keeps userspace as a controlled variable instead of removing it.** The
   contents are this unit's own binaries, unmodified. If the shell does not come
   up, the shell is not the new thing. Writing my own `init` would add a
   variable; the drop's `root.bin` would add three (different SDK generation,
   different model, not this unit's `H601`).
4. The plan's own stop-loss for this gate is *swap to an initramfs to halve the
   variables*. Starting there spends the halving instead of holding it, and what
   is held in reserve becomes the flash root — the **closer-to-vendor**
   direction, which is the better second experiment rather than the better first.

⚠️ **And Decision B has an arithmetic precondition that is checked before the
bench, not at it.** The image is entered at `0x80500000` and the kernel
decompresses to `0x80000000`, so the decompressed image must end below
`0x80500000` or the decompressor overwrites its own input: **5,242,880 bytes is
the ceiling.** This unit's decompressed kernel is 3,374,772 (量), leaving
**1,868,108 bytes** for everything the initramfs adds. If it does not fit, the
answer is not a smaller initramfs first — it is `LOAD_START_ADDR=0x80A00000`,
which `rtkload/Makefile` already supports for other boards and which `G0` has
already probed on this device.

| | question | `SPEC.md` |
|:-:|---|---|
| ⓐ | which toolchain rlxfw's own kernel is built with — **decided, with a refutation condition**, not preferred | `TC-05` |
| ⓑ | does the `vmlinux` this project builds actually run | `TC-d` |
| ⓒ | which `arch/rlx` `.S` files are assembled under `.set reorder`, and does any of them rely on gas inserting the load-delay `nop` | `TC-g` |
| ⓓ | the first artefact of this project that a stranger can watch working | `plan/ARTIFACTS.md` §2 |

### 🆕 The running order, which is NOT the step numbering, and why

**Written 2026-08-28 before `R3-4` was started, and it holds.** The steps are
executed **`R3-4` → `R3-5` → then back to `R3-2`'s stages 3–6**,
not in numeric order.

**The reason is that stages 3–6 build an image out of a kernel that does not
exist yet.** `R3-2` verified the pipeline's first two stages against the
vendor's own intermediates, which is what made it an instrument. Stages 3–6
— LZMA, `cvimg vmlinuxhdr`, the link at `LOAD_START_ADDR`, `nfjrom` —
consume **the** `vmlinux`, and the `vmlinux` `R3` will upload is the one `R3-4`
configures and `R3-5` fills. Running `nfjrom` first would have produced an
artefact that had to be thrown away and re-derived, and — worse — a
set of sizes and sha256s in the write-up that described a file nobody would ever
upload.

量 2026-08-28, and this is why it was not a close call: the `vmlinux` at the end of today is
**3,968,113** bytes where `R3-2` measured **3,340,287** — **627,826 bytes** of
difference, from `R3-4`'s flag (+33,248 on the file) and `R3-5`'s initramfs
(+594,578, of which 584,704 is the cpio in `.init.ramfs`). Every size and sha256 that
stages 3–6 would have produced this morning describes a file nobody will upload.

⚠️ The first version of this paragraph added `.text`'s +16,788 to the cpio's
584,704 and called the total 627,826. Those are a section delta and a file delta

### 🆕 Carried forward out of `R3-4`'s adversarial pass, 2026-08-28

**Five reviewers, thirty-eight findings, and the four below are the ones that
were not fixed the same day.** Everything else was: the numbers were re-derived,
the two wrong initramfs entries were removed, `kconfig-delta` gained six
controls and `mkinitramfs` seven, and `test-config-gates` gained the four
mutation classes that had none. These four are code changes to load-bearing
instruments and they get their own measurements.

| | what | why it is not fixed tonight |
|---|---|---|
| ~~**`TC-j`**~~ ✅ | 🔄 **Closed 2026-08-28/29.** `_scan_elf` is **deleted**, not left unused — a private copy of the pipeline that is still in the file is one somebody will call again. `K11`–`K16` now drive the **real CLI as a subprocess**, and a new `K17` is the control on the `HAZLINT_CHILD` guard they ride on. Not an in-process `main()`: that recurses, and the anti-recursion guard would itself be a path no user invocation takes — the same defect one level down. 🔴 **The three mutations `TC-j` named all passed 20/20 before and all fail now**: m17 (`--range` no longer refused) fails K11, m18 (`--vma-range` parsed and never clipping) fails K12, m19 (excision never runs) fails K13/K14/K16, and a fourth (m20, the guard doing more than suppressing) fails K13 — `test-hazlint.sh` **121 → 142**. **And moving them to the CLI immediately found three states `_scan_elf` accepted that the real program refuses**: a window intersecting nothing, a fixture whose every word is excised, and a `--vma-range` that missed its own site. The report now prints `unit` and `cli` as separate blocks and says **NO cli CONTROL RAN** if none did. ⚠️ `TC-j`'s claim that *CI runs zero hazlint cases* is true but is **not an oversight**: `K4`'s population control is 56 KiB of this unit's bootloader and can never be committed, so the suite exits 1 on a runner by design. 量: **all seven `cli` controls pass with `$FWRE_WORK` empty** — the part `TC-j` was about is the part that would run anywhere, and what keeps the row bench-only is `K4`/`K4b` |
| **`TC-k`** | **`mips16_gaps` reads a symbol set that excludes `st_size == 0` and `STT_NOTYPE`.** 量: this kernel has **51** such symbols in its executable sections — `__copy_user`, `ret_from_irq`, `restore_all`, `resume_userspace`, `bad_stack` among them. A fixture with a violating pair between two MIPS16 functions, labelled `STT_FUNC __copy_user st_size=0`, reports **0 violations, exit 0**; the same file with `st_size=8` reports 1. The docstring's *"would have to be absent from the symbol table of a kernel that is not stripped"* is false | today's exposure is placement luck: no size-0 or NOTYPE symbol currently lands in one of the 20 gaps (largest 88 B, 406 B total). The rule needs a size bound or a second source, and picking one needs measurement |
| **`TC-l`** | **A section marked `SHF_ALLOC` without `SHF_EXECINSTR` that holds code is dropped with a green verdict.** No coverage ratio, no threshold, no non-zero exit — just a `NOT SCANNED` line. 量: a fixture whose `.text` is ALLOC-only and holds the violating pair reports 0 violations, exit 0, where 1.3's segment basis reported 1 | mitigated today and measured: on all three builds no `STT_FUNC` is defined in any ALLOC-not-EXEC section, and `.dram` (the only candidate, 26,960 B) reads 0 loads under `--raw`. The fix is a coverage floor, which is a policy decision |
| **`TC-m`** | **On an `ET_REL` `.o` the excision is printed and does not happen.** Section addresses are 0 so spans get a synthetic base while the symbol readers return section-relative values; the holes never intersect and `_cut` cuts nothing, but `print_spans` prints *"EXCISED BY NAME"* anyway. Two contradictory claims in one report, and `notes/kernel-build.md` §2.1 proposes `hazlint` over `.o` files as a build step | 🔄 **2026-08-29: `P2` is no longer waiting on it, and the reason is a measurement.** `hazlint-objs`' `Q8` reads every object whose report claims an excision — **26 of them, 27 in the marked trees — and not one scanned fewer bytes than its `SHF_EXECINSTR` sections hold**. The claimed excision removes nothing, so those bytes are SCANNED rather than skipped: the error can manufacture a violation and cannot hide one, and a 0 from `P2` is not weakened by it. **Still not fixed, still carried** — a one-line guard that needs a control, and now it also needs a decision about what an `ET_REL` scan should do with MIPS16 it cannot locate |

**And two findings that are not `hazlint`'s.** Both are closed, 2026-08-28/29,
and both turned out to be described wrongly — which is now the third session
running where that is the headline.

**✅ `CONFIG_PRINTK`.** Decided: **two declared variants from one delta file**,
`quiet` (the vendor's configuration) and `loud` (`+CONFIG_PRINTK=y
+CONFIG_PRINTK_TIME=y`), both carrying the eleven boot marks, `loud` uploaded
first. 🔴 **§6.6's trap fired on the first attempt** — setting `PRINTK`
alone takes `(NEW)` from 0 to 1 — so `PRINTK_TIME` is pinned and `(NEW)` is back
to 0. Its value is a measurement, not a default: `arch/rlx` defines **no**
`sched_clock`, so `printk_time` uses the jiffies generic and **not** the CP0
`Count` this die does not implement. Cost 量: decompressed image **3,472,384 →
3,546,112, +73,728 (+2.1 %)**, 66.2 % → 67.6 % of the ceiling, `hazlint` 0 on
both. ⚠️ **The estimate written before the build was "150–300 KB" and it was
wrong by 2–4×.**

**✅ `grep -r` over `arch/rlx`.** The blind spot is real — **13 files, 91,549
bytes, the entire BSP** — and the positive control fires (`bsp_swcore_init`
`-r`=0 `-R`=1). **Fifteen zero-claims re-run and NOT ONE is refuted.** One
enumeration moves: `TC-g`'s *seventeen* `.S` files is **eighteen**, and the
eighteenth is a linker script that changes no count.
🔴 **But the instruction itself was wrong outside `arch/rlx`, and wrong in
the dangerous direction.** At the drop root `-R` reports **79,857 paths for
66,977 real files (+19.2 %)** and follows three `romfs/tmp -> /var/tmp` links
**out of the tree**: a planted canary came back as vendor content three times,
and four of this project's own `boa` analysis logs are reachable that way today.
🔴 **And the bigger finding**: `arch/rlx/bsp` **dangles in two of the three
drops** — `target` is a tracked symlink only in `rtl819x-toolchain` — so
re-running a BSP question with `-R` across the three returns 13/0/0 and a reader
would write down *"only one drop has it"*, which is false. The BSP is in all
three at `boards/rtl8196e/bsp/`, 12 files, byte-identical but for one `#if` in
`setup.c`'s reboot path. `notes/kernel-build.md` §10, `TC-27`, `TC-28`.

### 🆕 Carried forward out of `R3-7`, 2026-08-29

| **`TC-p`** 🆕 | **`SPEC.md` held two rows for one mechanism and they disagreed, and nothing in this repository compares two rows of the same table to each other.** `LDR-26` said `nfjrom` and `boot.img` both force `0x80000000`; `LDR-37`, eleven rows below it, said `boot.img` writes the address — and `LDR-37` is right (讀 twice, the second on this unit's own `stage2.bin`). **`spec-check.py` cannot see this class**: its C5 chases a row's value into the file that owns it, and both rows name owners that agree with them individually | a checker for *two rows describing one mechanism* needs a declaration of which rows are about the same thing, and inventing that declaration today would be a second owner of the table's structure. What was cheap and is done: `LDR-26` now points at `LDR-37` and the corrected row carries both readings |
| ~~**the socket**~~ ✅| **Which physical Ethernet socket the cable is in is recorded nowhere.** 讀 (not 推 — the source says it outright): `eth1` is the WAN, `RTL_DRV_WAN0_NETIF_NAME "eth1"`, and `eth0`/`eth2`/`eth3`/`eth4` are **four** LAN ports on vid 9 (`0x10`/`0x8`/`0x4`/`0x2`). `NET-13`'s jack↔port map is 未定 and has been withdrawn twice  ✅ **答了一格，2026-08-29，而且不是靠看機殼。** `L-6c`…`L-6f` 一次帶一個介面 ping：`eth0`–`eth3` 全部 4/4 失敗**而且主機端連 ARP request 都沒有**，`eth4` 通，4/4，0% loss，主機 `tcpdump -e` 同時有兩個方向 —— 而**來源 MAC 的尾碼就指名了是哪一個 netdev**，所以不是板子自己說的。`K6` 試五個而不是兩個正是這一列（與 §B5-c9）的功勞：四個 LAN 裡試兩個會**假否證 D5**。🔴 **我原本在這裡寫「這一列記的埠遮罩是錯的」，那是我錯了，兩半都錯。** 量 `upstream/dumps/uart-boot.log`：原廠是 `eth0=0x10`、`eth1=0x1`（WAN）、`eth2=0x8`、`eth3=0x4`、`eth4=0x2`，所以舊記錄逐一相符，而「漏掉」的 `0x1` 是 WAN 的。🔴 **兩個 build 對交換器的列舉是鏡像的，而那才是發現。** 量，並排看 —— `upstream/dumps/uart-boot.log` 的原廠開機對 `bench/2026-08-30b/L3.log`：原廠 `eth0=0x10 eth1=0x1(vid 8) eth2=0x8 eth3=0x4 eth4=0x2`，我的 `eth0=0x1 eth1=0x10(vid 8) eth2=0x2 eth3=0x4 eth4=0x8`。換成位元索引就是 **`我的 = 4 − 原廠`**，五個全部符合 —— 一次 5 位元反轉，`eth3` 在 bit 2 是固定的中點。**member port 的位元指的是實體交換器埠，那是硬體釘死的**，所以 netdev↔孔 的對應在兩個 build 之間不同，照 `NET-04` 寫的驅動在我的 kernel 下會驅動錯的孔。`RTL_WANPORT_MASK` 在不同 `#ifdef` 下同時有 `0x10` 與 `0x01` 兩個版本（讀，`rtl865x_netif.h:400`、`:411`），差異從那裡進來。⚠️ `NET-13` 的「機殼上五個孔怎麼排」仍然未定：今天答的是**線在哪**，不是**孔怎麼排**| it is a bench fact and this was a desk session. `K6` tries **all five in order** rather than assuming one, so the cost of not knowing is four typed commands rather than a power cycle. 🔴 **The first version of this row said `K6` tests two candidates, which would have refuted D5 on a cable in any of three LAN jacks** — the adversarial pass caught it. **If all five are silent that is TWO results** — D5 refuted and the socket still unknown — and the write-up must not collapse them. 🆕 Free: the six netdev MACs are distinct SDK placeholders `00:12:34:56:78:9x` and the last octet names the netdev, so `tcpdump -e` on one successful ping answers half of `NET-13` |
| **`--sweep`'s 32** 🆕 | **32 of the 168 predicted cells in `bench/` have no capture and the sweep does not say which**, only how many. 量 2026-08-29: 38 files, 168 cells, 136 ordered, 0 out of order | twelve of the 32 are tomorrow's, and the rest are seatings that stopped where they stopped — which the per-file check already names. A `--sweep --verbose` is one loop and it would be a second way to print what `check` prints; it goes in when a reader actually needs the list rather than the count |
| **`hardware watchpoint`** 🆕 | **A seventh `/proc/cpuinfo` field this unit's shipped kernel prints and no build from these drops can** (量: the format string occurs once in `vmlinux-rederived.bin`, zero times in either of my images) — a second data point of `TC-17`'s shape | it is recorded as `TC-36` and used as a discriminator. **Turning it into an argument about WHICH tree the shipped kernel came from needs the same corpus work `which-drop.md` did for binaries**, and one format string is not that |

### 🆕 Carried forward out of `R1h-3` / `R3-8a`, 2026-08-29 — the seating

**Four, and three of them are defects in instruments rather than open questions
about the device.** Everything the seating could fix in the same session was
fixed: `SPEC.md`'s nine rows, the owner files, `tools/rbcheck.py`, and
`bench/2026-08-30/CORRECTIONS-block0.md` for the frozen block.

| | what | why it is not fixed today |
|---|---|---|
| **`--seconds`** 🆕 | 🔴 **`console-capture.py capture` with neither `--seconds` nor `--idle` never returns** — both default to `0.0` and the read loop (`:543-551`) breaks on neither. 量: `rc=124` under `timeout -s TERM 8`. A kill loses `.meta.json`; the `.log` and `.timing` survive, flushed per chunk. **14 of §B5's 15 capture rows omit it and block 0 omits it on `Q-A`, while all ten committed `A-catch` captures passed one** 🔴 **— and that census is an inference, because `.meta.json` records neither `seconds` nor `idle`, which is the larger defect and the one to fix first** | the fix is a refusal when neither is given — three lines — but it changes **the one instrument the entire seating runs through**, and `test-console-capture.sh` would have to be re-run and probably amended. **Changing an instrument between its desk validation and the bench is the shape `P7` exists to prevent.** 🔄 **That reason expired when the seating ended, so here is the one that replaces it, with the measurement whoever does it will need**: 量 2026-08-30, `tools/test-console-capture.sh` holds **four** real capture invocations with no terminator (`:196`, `:307`, `:315`, `:332`), and every one of them passes `--port /dev/null` while expecting a **different** refusal — a malformed `--send`, a 128-byte line, a non-ASCII line. **A terminator check placed before those validations changes their error message and turns four passing cases red for the wrong reason.** So the refusal has to sit after them and before the port is opened, and it needs its own case plus a negative one. That is a step, not a three-line edit, and it goes in at the desk before the next seating |
| **`G0`'s pointer shape** 🆕 | 🔴 **A refutation condition that fires on noise.** *"Any one pointer-shaped word and the address is re-picked"*, on 64 words of uniform random data, fires with probability 1−(63/64)⁶⁴ = **63.6 %**; the *aligned* reading fires at **22.2 %**. 2026-08-29 was its first run, and two of the 64 arena words carried a listed prefix (neither aligned) | rewriting it needs a decision about what the condition is actually for. **A rate would work** — *more than k pointer-shaped words in n* — but picking k needs the null measured on this board's own bias rather than assumed uniform, and `MEM-16` already says this DRAM's bias is 89.5 % reproducible against a measured null of 55.98 %, so uniform is the wrong model to calibrate against |
| **the `^[` attribution** 🆕 | The encoding difference is 量 in both directions — the loader echoes raw `0x1B` (81,457 in one capture, 117 in another) and `24d`/`24e`/`24f` hold caret pairs and no raw ESC. **That `^[` comes from a Linux tty with `echoctl` is 推**, resting on those three being the later cycles of the day the vendor kernel was booted to userspace | settling it means reading the vendor rootfs's termios setup or catching it live, and neither is worth a power cycle. **It is recorded as 推 and the rule built on it is stated in the corrected form**, which is what `CORRECTIONS-block0.md` §2 does |
| **`L6c-up` and friends** 🆕 | **Four captures nobody predicted.** §B5's `L-6c` is written as two commands in one row (`ifconfig <prev> down` then `ifconfig <next> … up`); each is a `console-capture.py` invocation, so each is a `.log`, and the second needed a name | `--sweep` walks predictions to captures and **never the other way**, so these sit outside the ordering discipline — the same gap already recorded for `CONT3` and for `--sweep`'s 32. The fix is the same unbuilt one: a sweep that also walks captures back to predictions and names the orphans |
| **`probe3`'s bitmap** 🆕 | 🔴 **The block advertises a retained bitmap it does not hold.** `probe3.c:1324-1329` says the boundary point's pattern *"survives to the read-back"*; 量 2026-08-30, `bmp_clear()` runs again at `:1385`, `:1667` and `:1808`, all after the boundary rerun at `:1336`, so the region holds `x-c10`'s two victims while `bmp.count` still advertises 512. Nothing published is falsified — `bmp.rerun.fresh` is computed before the clobber — but **the pattern was the only evidence that could have shown the 20 FRESH victims arriving in pairs `{k, k+256}`, the direct fingerprint of two ways sharing a set** | it is a `probe3` change, and `probe3` is not rebuilt between seatings without re-running `P7` and the whole `hazlint` gate. `tools/rbcheck.py` now **reports** the discrepancy and deliberately does not fail on it. The fix is either to move the retained point after the last `bmp_clear()` or to give it its own region; both need a control, and the block layout is `docs/probe3-cells.md` §4's |
| **`.meta.json`'s terminator** 🆕 | 🔴 **`console-capture.py` does not record its own `--seconds`/`--idle`.** 讀 `:302-333`: the metadata carries `esc_seconds`, `esc_after_seconds`, `esc_period_requested_s` and `cr_settle_s` — and neither terminator. **So today's census of "every committed `A-catch` passed `--seconds`" is an inference from the `stop_reason` string, which is one-directional**: it can prove the flag was passed and never that it was not | this is the **larger** of the two `--seconds` defects and it goes in with the refusal, in the same step: recording a field and refusing on its absence are one change to one dict plus one guard. The tool's own comment at `:217-219` says *"what the instrument did to produce it is part of the reading"*, which is the argument for doing it |
| **`w-assoc`'s M(T) table** 🆕 | **The associativity cell reports only the argmin `(T, M)`, and the argmin is not the proof — the shape of `M` over `T` is.** 量 today: `(8192, 3)`, from which 16 KiB two-way is the unique fit, but only because the alternatives were re-derived at the desk. The run itself discarded `M` at 2048, 4096 and 16384 | one extra result word would carry the whole curve, and it costs nothing on the wire. ⚠️ And `probe3.c:1362-1365` claims **two** free controls where only the `m==1` one is implemented (`:1390-1395`); *"the largest M at the smallest T must show FRESH"* is checked nowhere and its outcome is not recoverable from the output |

### Refutation condition, written now

**The gate.** `R3` is not closed by a shell prompt. It is closed by a capture in
which **D1 through D5 all hold in one boot** and the discriminator string that
only my tree emits is present. Three of five is `R3a`, recorded as such.

**Decision A is refuted by** any of: `rsdk-1.3.6-4181` failing to build an object
of the `R3` configuration; `R3-1` finding that the four unexplained violations in
the 4181 `vmlinux` are **compiler output** on an executed path; or a boot failure
whose signature is a load-use hazard at a compiler-generated site. It is **not**
refuted by the shipped image being a different toolchain generation — `TC-18`
measured that channel as an instrument for code generators, and rlxfw is not
reproducing the shipped image.

**Decision B is refuted by** the initramfs failing to unpack, by `arch/rlx`
needing a source change to support it, or by the decompressed image exceeding
5,242,880 bytes with `LOAD_START_ADDR = 0x80500000`. The first two are findings
in their own right, because the vendor's own build system advertises this path;
the third has a known answer (`0x80A00000`) and is arithmetic, not an experiment.

**`R3-3` is refuted in the direction that matters by a channel that works.** If
the desk channel runs my kernel to a shell, that is **not** D1–D5 and the write-up
says so: *"runs in an emulator"* and *"runs on this silicon" are two claims, and
`R1a` has not moved.*

### Stop-loss, written now

- **Five boot attempts that do not reach D2** → stop the ladder and run the
  plan's halving experiment: the **vendor's** kernel with **my** initramfs. That
  splits *my kernel* from *my image* in one power cycle.
- **Two seatings without reaching D4** → the gate splits: D1–D3 close as `R3a`
  and D4–D5 move to their own gate, rather than a gate being held open.
- **Any capture showing a flash-write path taken** → power off, and the write-up
  starts from the capture.
- **`R3-3` costing more than one desk segment** → it stops and records *no desk
  execution channel*, with what was tried. It is an instrument, not a gate.

✅ **Closed 2026-08-29.** `R1h-3` ran on power cycle 1 of seating 5 and `R1h-4`

both of 否證 ⓐ's controls firing in both directions), **ⓒ closes positive**
(four `cache` ops retire while `x ri` traps in the same run under the same
handler), **ⓑ is 未定 after one of the two seatings the stop-loss allows**
(`c-A` negative, and the payload's own interlock voided Group V rather than
reporting the voided cells as passes), and **ⓓ's second half has a reading**
(none of the three bits sticks, and the refutation condition did *not* fire, so
the reading carries information). No flash-write command was issued; the
flash-byte count is **unmeasured**, because no `FLR` bracket was run.
🔴 **The gate stayed open
across `R2` and the whole of `R3`'s desk work, which the re-cut of 2026-08-26
said was deliberate rather than stalled — and it was: what it was waiting for
was a power cycle it was always going to share.**

**Written 2026-08-26 on entering the gate, before any of it was done.** `R1h`
exists because `R1-gate` closed with three residuals and **an item with no owning
gate is a bug in this file's own list** — the same rule that caught `C-16` being
orphaned by `R0`. Budget **猜, uncalibrated: 4 segments** — 2 desk, 1 instrument,
1 bench, and **the bench segment is shared with `R3`'s seating rather than being
its own power cycle.**

🔴 **RE-CUT 2026-08-26, after the desk half was written.** The seating is at the
**tail of `R3`**, so `R1h`'s desk half and `R1h`'s bench half are separated by
the whole of `R2` and `R3` — and three of the five steps were written assuming
they were adjacent. **`R1h`'s desk work ends at `R1h-1`. `R1h-2`, `R1h-3` and
`R1h-4` move to `R3`'s seating preparation** and are marked ⏸ below.

**Why `R1h-2` could not stay where it was**: it produces *"the seating sheet
section, written into `RUNSHEET.md` **beside `R3`'s**"*, and `R3`'s section will
not exist for ~12 segments; its DoD (`check-predictions.py` passing on a block
written before the first capture) is a **bench-time** artefact and cannot be met
at the desk at all. A step whose DoD cannot be reached is not a step.

⚠️ **So `R1h` stays OPEN across `R2` and `R3`, and that is deliberate rather than
stalled.** Written down because the next reader of this file — including me in a
few months — will otherwise read a long-open gate as a stuck one. The gate still
owns ⓐ ⓑ ⓒ ⓓ; what moved is when its bench half is spent.

🔴 **And the ordering INSIDE that seating does not move, whatever the scheduling
does.** `probe3` runs **first**, before `R3`'s kernel. `R3`'s DoD is *my kernel
boots to a shell and pings* — and in that state the loader is gone, the DRAM is
gone, there is no `<RealTek>` prompt to type `J` into and no `DW` to recover the
result block. **"At the tail of `R3`" is when the seating happens, not the order
within it.**

**What `R1h` exists to settle**, so that closing it can be checked against

| ⓓ | write-through or write-back-without-write-allocate; and are `Status.IsC`/`SwC` implemented as bits | `CPU-19` 殘留 |

Empty the build directory, rebuild, record the `sha256`, and `rb=80a02000` is the stale-build check. **In the file, not in anyone's memory** | Runs to its own end under `qemu-system-mips`; **the mutation for each cell produces the failure that cell exists to detect**; the result block is recoverable with one `DW` after the payload's own watchdog reset | 🔴 **qemu will disagree with the device again, and that is the expected case.** `probe1`'s cell 1 came back FRESH on qemu and STALE on silicon. **A qemu run that looks like the device is the run to distrust**, and every cell needs its expected-under-qemu value written down separately from its expected-on-device value |
| ✅ **`R1h-2`** | desk ½, **at `R3`'s seating prep** | 🔄 **Closed 2026-08-29 with `R3-7`, which is the same step.** `RUNSHEET` §B5's card, its **eleven** corrections (§B5-c1…c12, and `c12` is the list of what the frozen block says that is now known to be wrong), and `bench/2026-08-30b/PREDICTIONS-B5-block1.md` — twelve cells, frozen, `0 of 12` at the desk because control `N2` fires on every capture that is still in the future. ⚠️ **`R1h-3`'s own block is NOT written**, and that is deliberate: `probe3` is power cycle 1 and its cells are `R1h-3`'s to predict. *(as written on entering:)* **The seating sheet section, written into `RUNSHEET.md` beside `R3`'s** — including **two separate uploads**, because the loader re-stages `0x80500000` on a watchdog reset and one power cycle cannot run two payloads without re-uploading | `tools/check-predictions.py` passes on a block written before the first capture | **The order.** `R3` is the expensive one and `probe3` is the cheap one; if `R3` boots and takes the board away, `probe3` has to have run first — or the seating buys one thing |
| ✅ **`R1h-3`**| bench 1, **the tail of `R3`** — 🔄 **desk half done 2026-08-29** | **The run**, on `R3`'s power cycle, **and `probe3` goes first within it**. 🔄 **The desk half was not in this row when it was written and it turned out to be half a segment of its own**: `bench/2026-08-30/PREDICTIONS-B5-block0.md`, thirteen cells, frozen, `0 of 13`, **and its §0 is the card** because `RUNSHEET` §B5's card excludes power cycle 1 in its own sentence. `P7` is spent — `probe3` rebuilt 2026-08-29, sha256 byte-identical to `R1h-1`'s. **What remains is the seating**  ✅ **收掉 2026-08-29 晚上。** ⓐ 有量測（16 KiB / 16 B / 2-way，兩個控制在兩個方向都成立）、ⓒ 正向收（四條 `cache` 全 retire，而 `x ri` 在同一次 run 陷入）、ⓑ 記為未定（`c-A` 否定，Group V 被 payload 自己的連鎖 void，停損允許第二次上機）、ⓓ 後半有讀數（三個位元都沒留住，而否證條件沒觸發）。**零 flash 位元組。** DoD 的「兩個通道一致」現在是算術：`tools/rbcheck.py` 三路全部 `C93E60B5`，三個 margin 字都是 `DEADC0DE`，十個控制成立。🔴 **而這一列預言的失敗模式今天沒有發生，另一種發生了**：它寫「桌面那半的失敗模式是引用沒動過的來源、抄沒重量的控制」，今天兩者都沒犯 —— 犯的是**照著一張沒有人真的跑過的卡片**，而那是在通電前讀 `argparse` 抓到的，不是在板邊| ⓐ ⓑ ⓒ each get a reading or an explicit 未定 with the reason; **zero flash bytes**; the result block agrees on both channels — 🔄 **and that last clause is now mechanical rather than an eyeball**: the UART's `sum=`, the seal word, and a re-sum of the recovered block **minus `0x10`** (for `progress(P_SEALED)`'s re-stamp) must all agree, a procedure demonstrated on `probe2`'s committed `H2g`/`H2a` with its negative control firing before it was written down | **A fault ends the seating and takes `R3` with it.** `probe3` runs before `R3`, and every cell writes its result before the next cell starts. 🔄 **And the desk half's own failure mode showed up twice**: a value predicted from a previous payload's reading without checking whether the source file had moved since (`install.changed`), and a control quoted from a frozen block without re-measuring it (`A-catch`'s missing negative). Both were caught by the adversarial pass, neither by re-reading the draft |
| ✅ **`R1h-4`**| desk ½, **after that seating** | **The write-up.** `CPU-25`, `CPU-44`, `CPU-45` and `CPU-19` 殘留 land; `C-6`'s remaining half closes or is restated again; `R1-gate`'s decision ② gets its answer in `docs/rlx-cache-and-cp0.md`  ✅ **收掉 2026-08-29，與上機同一個 commit。** `CPU-25`（大小/line/關聯度，量）、`CPU-44`（retire，正向）、`CPU-45`（未定，第一次上機）、`CPU-19` 殘留（② 收、① 跟著 `CPU-45` 走）全部落地，`docs/rlx-cache-and-cp0.md` § ② 給出的是**下一個實驗**而不是一段論證。`tools/spec-check.py` 九個控制先成立然後全綠。🔴 **而這一列預言的陷阱正是今天踩到的東西 —— 只是踩在別人腳上**：kernel 印的 `icache: 16kB/16B, dcache: 8kB/16B` 每一個數字都是 `bspcpu.h` 的 `#define`，是「穿著量測外衣的建置常數」的教科書例子。**走訪的數字與 kernel 的數字是兩個宣稱，而這份寫上去在它們一致的時候也這樣說了** —— 同一行還帶著 `dcache: 8kB`，而那個**完全沒有量測**| `tools/spec-check.py` clean; **decision ② names a measurement or names the next experiment**, and does not name an argument | The same trap `R1g-5` walked into: a geometry number that is a build constant wearing a measurement's clothes. **The walk's number and the kernel's number are two different claims** and the write-up says so even when they agree |

### Refutation condition, written now

> **否證 ⓐ** — the eviction walk's own negative control is a victim set smaller
> than any plausible cache: **every victim must come back STALE**. If a victim
> reports FRESH at a working-set size no cache could evict from, the walk is
> measuring something that is not capacity eviction and **the size number is
> void, not approximate**.

> **否證 ⓑ** — 🔴 **NARROWED 2026-08-26: cell E is cell A's positive control in
> only ONE of its two branches, and the form below is too strong.**
> `docs/probe3-cells.md` § 6.5 and `docs/rlx-cache-and-cp0.md` § ② own the full
> statement. If E shows a **held** store, the line was resident, read-allocate is
> real, and A's *fresh* can only mean the alias is snooped — eviction being
> excluded by the far-apart pair. **If E shows write-through, it says nothing
> about residency**: ⓓ is answered in passing and A's *fresh* stays a two-way
> disjunction whose survivors no observation through the alias can separate.
> 🔴 **And the dependency runs both ways**: E assumes its own middle load
> allocated, which is what A measures — so a negative A voids E too.
> *(Kept as written:)*
> **cell E is the positive control for cell A.** If E cannot produce
> a dirty line — that is, if a store to a line the CPU has just loaded is
> immediately visible uncached — then this core is write-through after all, cell
> A's *fresh* has one fewer explanation, and **ⓓ is answered in passing.** If E
> *does* produce one, then A's *fresh* would mean the alias is snooped, and that
> is a result about the alias rather than about the cache.

> **否證 ⓒ** — a `cache` instruction that neither retires nor traps (the payload
> hangs) refutes the handler, not the instruction. `probe3` writes its cell
> result **before** issuing the instruction, exactly as `probe1` does.

### Stop-loss, written now

| If | then |
|---|---|
| **Two seatings** and cell A still cannot be made to hold | `CPU-45` is recorded **未定**, and **`R6` carries the conservative cost**: rings *and* payload buffers in the uncached window, which is what the vendor's own driver does for the rings. The throughput number in `R6`'s DoD is then measured against that, and is not compared with anything |
| `cache 0x11` traps | **That is an answer, not a failure.** `CPU-44` closes negative, the D-invalidate candidates reduce to `CCTL 0x001`/`0x200`, and **this unit's own kernel becomes a puzzle worth its own row** — it contains 37 instructions its own silicon will not execute |
| The eviction walk returns a size that is not a power of two | Record **未定** rather than the number. A non-power-of-two is the walk measuring something else, and rounding it to the nearest plausible value is how a build constant gets laundered into a measurement |
| `R3` and `probe3` cannot both fit one seating | **`probe3` goes first.** It is the cheap one, and `R3` booting a kernel is what ends a seating |

## `R2a/b/d`'s step list — closed 2026-08-28, kept as written

**Written 2026-08-27 on entering the gate, before any of it was done.** Budget
**猜, uncalibrated: 6 segments**, all desk, none of it needing power. The gate
board's estimate says 5; the plan's §12.1 says 6. Neither is calibrated and the
difference is not worth resolving before the first step reports.

🔴 **What the first hour of this gate refuted, and it was in this file.**
§ Blocked on said on 2026-08-26 that the reproducible-build container gates
`R2c` and that *"`R2a/b/d` needs none of it"*. It does. `plan/router-rebuild-plan.md:1084`
heads that container with **"前置 —— 可重現的建置環境（1 個工作段，`R2a` 之前）"**,
and the reason is in the step definitions: `R2a` **rebuilds BusyBox 1.13.4 with
T-vendor** and `R2b` **builds each of three drops' `boa`**. Both need the 32-bit
`rsdk` running. The narrowing had taken the gate board's *"none of it needs
`R1`"* and read it as *none of it needs the container*; those are two different
sentences about two different things.

**What is actually startable without the container** — and it is most of the
value: the **six-tree similarity matrix**, whose material is already on disk
(量 2026-08-27: six `boa` at 526,732 / 522,556 / 509,632 / **485,012 ← this
unit** / 404,904 / 400,424 bytes, matching the plan's table row for row, and six
`busybox` beside them), and **`R2d`'s two greps**. So the gate opens there, and
the container becomes a step of this gate rather than a wall in front of it.

| | question | `SPEC.md` |
|:-:|---|---|
| ⓐ | which GPL drop this firmware was built from — **decided by a metric with a stated floor**, not by a resemblance | `TC-02` — 🔄 **and `-1` answered half of it**: the floor exists and is stated (`boa unit-2018` against `busybox unit-2018` = 0.1581, the `CROSS` cell itself), the matrix places this unit, and `TC-02` still cannot move off 推 from shipped images alone. `TC-12`, `TC-02a` |
| ⓑ | what the vendor kernel emulates, and with what cache-management model | `CPU-44` 殘留, `C-7`/`F51` |
| ⓒ | a reproducible build environment that can run `rsdk` at all — **and it is `P4a`'s precondition too**, which is why it belongs to a gate rather than to a session | `TC-05` |

| Step | | What it produces | DoD | Where it is most likely to be wrong |
|---|---:|---|---|---|
| **`R2a/b/d-0`** ✅ | desk 1 | **The similarity metric itself, with its controls before its results.** `binsim`, and the `BASE`/`FLOOR` thresholds computed from the six trees rather than chosen | The plan's own failure condition is evaluated and **printed**: *if all fifteen pairwise scores span < 5 percentage points the metric has no discrimination and its numbers are void*. Plus a positive control (a file against itself = 1.0) and a negative one (a file against `/dev/urandom` of the same length) | **Size dominates.** The six `boa` run 400,424 to 526,732 bytes and any metric with a length term measures config, not toolchain. 量 2026-08-27, and the second reading is the one that matters: `v2.1.2` and `n300rt-2.1.6`'s `busybox` are the **same size** and **differ in exactly 8 bytes, all of them digits in the BusyBox banner's build date** — 99.997 % identical, one build rebuilt on another day. So `busybox` has **five** independent samples across the six trees, not six; `boa` has six. That pair is the free calibration point: a metric that does not put it at the very top is not measuring structure, and a metric that puts everything there is not measuring anything | ✅ **2026-08-27**: `tools/binsim.py` (32 controls, `k=7` chosen by a pre-registered rule), `tools/binsim-corpus.tsv`, `tools/test-binsim.sh` (71 cases), `notes/binsim.md`. The failure condition was evaluated and printed: `boa` spans **92.8 pp**, `busybox` **83.5 pp**, `acltd` **0.0 pp** and the void verdict fires there, which is the verdict's own positive control. 🔴 **And the step refuted the plan's `FLOOR`**: 0.0650 against a cross-program floor of 0.1581.
| **`R2a/b/d-1`** ✅ | desk 1 | **The matrix**: fifteen pairwise scores over six real builds, `boa` and `busybox` separately. **Nobody has done this** | Every cell has a number, `BASE` and `FLOOR` are named cells of that matrix, and the two are separated by more than the metric's own noise. 🔴 **2026-08-27 — `-0` changed this DoD before this step started.** The separation is not in doubt: `BASE` 0.9818 against `FLOOR` 0.0650 over a noise floor of 0.0000. 🔄 **That noise floor was refuted the same day it was written** — the pairs it came from are selected *by* the byte-equality of the window they are then scored on, so 1.000 is arithmetic; the estimate is 8.0e-4 and it is the smallest of three candidates. The DoD sentence is left as written because this column is the record of what the step was asked to do. What is in doubt is `FLOOR` itself, which sits **below** `CROSS` = 0.1581, the score of two DIFFERENT PROGRAMS from one tree. So this step's first output is not a reading of the clusters — it is **a named replacement floor at or above `CROSS`**, written into `tools/binsim-corpus.tsv`'s `@floor` row, after which `binsim --corpus` stops exiting 1. Until then the plan's three-band rule has a middle band that carries no information. ✅ **2026-08-27**: `@floor` = `boa unit-2018` against `busybox unit-2018` = **0.1581**, the `CROSS` cell itself, in a five-field cross-program form the manifest grew for it. 🔴 **The step's first answer was `busybox unit-2018 v3.4.0` = 0.1646 and the adversarial review killed it on the denominator**: containment divides by the smaller feature set, busybox's is 1.46× boa's, and at a matched denominator the ordering reverses — a pair sharing its whole upstream source across the model change scores 0.1212, below the 0.1551–0.1581 a pair sharing no source reaches. So at this scale no cell sits above the no-shared-source level and below `BASE`, and the tightest correct floor *is* that level. `E7` asserts it is the top of a three-program population at one denominator; `E8` measures what a model change alone costs, which turns the rule's `VOID` precondition into a reading. `--corpus` exits 0. `notes/which-drop.md` owns the decision, the rejected candidates, and the reading. **`TC-02` stays 推** — the corpus holds six shipped images and none of them is a GPL drop | 🔄 **2026-08-27, and this column was the one that was wrong.** It said *"reading a cluster as a toolchain when it is a product line … date and product are confounded with SDK"*. **Product line is crossed with the clustering, not confounded with it**, and the corpus refutes it on its own: 讀 `/etc/version`, N150RT is in all three clusters, two different products inside one cluster score 0.9863/0.9818, and two builds of one product across clusters score 0.0650. What stayed collinear is date and SDK generation, which is a tautology. The way this step could still have gone wrong is the one it did not: **reading "which cluster" as "which drop"** — six shipped images cannot name a source release, and §5 of the note says so rather than reaching |
| **`R2a/b/d-2`** ✅ | desk 1 | **`R2d`, the two greps** — which instructions the vendor kernel emulates, and its cache-management model. Five minutes of work whose value is that it is *this unit's* kernel | Each grep has a positive control that fires, in the sense `notes/cache-model.md` already had to learn: a scanner that finds nothing in one file must be shown finding something in another | **The zero.** The `cache`-instruction scan already returned a false zero once by being run on `stage2.bin` alone (Corrections, 2026-08-26). The emulation grep is the same shape. 🔴 **And it happened again, twice, in this step** — once when a survey of the board configs reported `ULS=0` for `rtl8196e` because that board has no plain `config.linux-2.6.30`, only five suffixed variants, and `grep -l` on an absent file says nothing; once when the `--mips16` test reported a clean `boa` because `--base 0` was wrong for a binary that loads at `0x00400000`, so its `jal` control was 0/515 in range too. Both were caught by asking why a control was also zero, and both are now cases. ✅ **2026-08-27**: `notes/vendor-kernel-isa.md` (new, the owner), `tools/isa-probe.sh` + `tools/test-isa-probe.sh` (40 cases), `opcount.py --pairs` and `--mips16`, `hazlint` **1.3** (K10, K4b, and a correction to 1.2's own note), `SPEC.md` `CPU-47`/`CPU-48` and nine amended rows, and `CLAUDE.md`'s core-naming ban lifted on the `PRId` table it named as its own condition |
| **`R2a/b/d-3`** ✅ | desk 1 | **The container decision**, made and written down: Docker Desktop's WSL integration / `apt install docker.io` in the distro / `debootstrap` + a pinned `snapshot.debian.org` chroot with no daemon | `rsdk-linux-gcc --version` prints `4.4.5` inside it, and the recipe is in a file rather than in a shell history. 🔴 **2026-08-28 — this DoD was replaced before it was met, because `-2` had already fired it without doing the work.** The replacement is a real build: `tools/tc-smoke.sh`'s four rungs for every toolchain found, and above them a complete `vmlinux`. ✅ Met: three rsdk releases reach L4; `users/dhrystone` builds and runs with all three and every self-check value matches; **724 objects and a linked `vmlinux` from two different toolchains, rc=0 both times**. The recipe is in `notes/vendor-toolchains.md` §3 with pinned versions and sha256s | 量 2026-08-27: the distro has **no `docker`, no `podman`, no `debootstrap`**; the only `docker` on `PATH` is Docker Desktop's `/mnt/c` shim, which `LOG.md` 2026-08-24 records reporting *could not be found in this WSL 2 distro*. Route ① is the one already measured not to work. 🔴 **2026-08-27, from `-2`: the whole premise is refuted.** Both rsdk toolchains run natively in this distro — `rsdk-1.3.6-4181`'s gcc prints `3.4.6-1.3.6` and `rsdk-1.5.5-5281`'s prints `4.4.5-1.5.5p4`, exit 0, no container — so **this step's own DoD signal fired without any of its three routes**. What is left is not a decision: `rsdk-1.5.5`'s `as` misses exactly one 32-bit library, i386 `libz.so.1`, with `/lib32/libc.so.6` and `/lib/ld-linux.so.2` already present. **The step is not ticked**, because a step whose premise was refuted has not been performed; it is reshaped, and the residual is named rather than left as a wall 🔴 **2026-08-28: two of the three ways it could have been wrong, were.** The residual was recorded as *one* missing library for *`as`*; it is **seventeen binutils programs**, and the reason `--version` looked fine is that gcc/cpp/xgcc are statically linked. And the step was framed as supplying a dependency; the thing it actually produced is `TC-15` — the vendor's own compiler and assembler agreeing on which Lexra cores expose the load delay slot, and the measured cost of building this 4181 board with the 5281-configured rsdk. ⚠️ **Not proved**: the `vmlinux` built here has never been run, on the device or in an emulator |

Every build now re-stages from `src-vendor/`; 389 objects `file`-checked, 0 not big-endian MIPS |

### Refutation condition, written now

> **否證 ⓐ** — the metric's own null: if the fifteen pairwise scores span less
> than 5 percentage points, **no drop is identified and `TC-02` stays 推**. The
> number that would prove the identification wrong is a `BASE` that a *different*
> drop also clears; the matrix is what makes that visible instead of invisible.

🔴 **2026-08-27, `-1`: that condition did NOT fire, and `TC-02` stays 推 anyway.**
The span is 92.8 pp, twenty times the bar. Recorded here rather than quietly
skipped past, because a refutation condition that is satisfied and still leaves
the answer undetermined is saying that it was not the binding constraint. The
binding one was never written down and is now: **a similarity matrix over six
shipped images cannot name a source-and-toolchain release, whatever it scores.**
The condition above is about the *instrument*; `TC-02` is about the *material*.
`notes/which-drop.md` §5.

> **否證 ⓑ** — a grep returning zero is a claim. It is refuted by the same
> scanner finding a hit in a second artefact, and if it cannot, the zero is
> recorded as *not looked for* rather than as *not there*.

### Stop-loss, written now

| If | then |
|---|---|
| The metric has no discrimination after two attempts | `TC-02` stays **推**, and `R3` proceeds on the drop it already uses. **This gate blocks nothing** — that is on the gate board and it is why this is a stop-loss rather than a crisis |
| All three container routes fail | `R2a`/`R2b`'s rebuild halves are recorded **未定**, the matrix and `R2d` still stand on their own, and the container moves to `P4a` where it is load-bearing rather than convenient |
